i.e. as opposed to an encyclopedia

Cultivation principles focus on transforming ambient energy, or Qi, into spiritual energy (which is dependent on the individual). This spiritual energy can then be channeled into different techniques and power

Some concepts and cultivation methods differ, so what could be essential for one might be trivial for another.

碧 (bì): green jade/bluish green/blue/jade, 水潭 (shuǐtán): puddle/pool. Might also be translated as Bishui Pool

Actually something along the lines of “how strange!” or “how rare!” but JY is teasing the kids by repeating something they said earlier.

Chapter 71: Unintentional Timing

Ji Yuan finished a joke and looked at the two children who had come over. Yes, he had seen them clearly in his vision.

The two of them were dressed in clean, light blue robes without any speck of dust. Even their exposed boots were spotless, and their faces were clean.

They had come from the mountain road, and it was already dark. Would two ordinary kids dare to run into the mountains at this time? Not to mention coming to this kind of deep pool that was terrifying just to look at?

After looking behind him, he confirmed that there were no adults around. The probability that these two children were ordinary people was greatly reduced, but there was no demonic nor yin energy in their bodies…

‘The Mountain God? Or maybe this is the first time I am meeting real immortal cultivators after having been in this world for so long? ’

Ji Yuan’s heart was slightly moved, but he didn’t seem to be as excited as he imagined he would be before. He pretended to turn back and continue reading, but he was very curious about the specific backgrounds of the visitors.

Although Ji Yuan was calm and composed, the two children couldn’t bear it any longer. The boy said:

“Hey, fisherman, when are you going to leave? You won’t catch any fish anyway.”

The girl immediately added a sentence:

“It’s getting dark, aren’t you afraid of wild animals in the mountains?”

From the perspective of ordinary people, it was actually quite interesting for the two children to ask this question.

Ji Yuan turned to look at them again.

“It’s getting dark, you two children are still wandering deep in the mountains. Aren’t you afraid that your family will worry about you, or that wild beasts will eat you?”

“We are not afraid!”

“Yes! We are not afraid!”

As if to make her statement more convincing, the girl added another sentence.

“Don’t look down on us because we’re small. We have high martial arts skills!”

Ji Yuan smiled and nodded in agreement.

“I see. My apologies. But I am not afraid. I also have high martial arts skills!”

After saying this, Ji Yuan turned his head back to his book. Either way, he had no intention of moving.

Judging from their brief exchange, it seemed that these two children were really the same age that they appeared. They were not people who looked like children while in reality being eighty to one hundred years old.

“Hmph, you won’t catch any fish even if you fish all night long!”

As soon as the boy finished saying this, Ji Yuan’s expression suddenly changed. Although the fishing rod didn’t move, the hook in the pool seemed to have been touched.

The next moment, the fishing line trembled slightly, Ji Yuan narrowed his eyes and swung his wrist. Although there was no big movement, the fishing rod bent like magic and swung upwards.

“Crash!”

The originally calm turquoise water was broken by a string of splashes. A small silver-white translucent fish about the length of an index finger was hooked on the fishhook and thrown into the air along the direction of the fishing line and rod.

“The Silver Essence!”1

The boy and girl exclaimed in unison.

With a shout, the boy almost subconsciously pulled out a circular blue jade pendant from his sleeve. In an instant, the blue jade grew to a large size, leaving a faint blue trail of light as it flew towards the silver fish that was still hanging in the air. The light formed a vague outline of a bag behind the jade pendant.

“Huh!?”

Ji Yuan swung the bamboo pole, and with the skills of a top martial arts master, the fishing line that held the silver fish moved as flexibly as a flying bird. The blue jade did not fly slowly, but it could not catch the silver fish.

After the jade pendant brushed past the silver fish twice, Ji Yuan simply swung the rod downwards, and the silver fish attached to the hook at the end of the fishing line suddenly dove down towards Ji Yuan.

A line of water rose from the pool and condensed into a sphere of water the size of a rubber ball in front of Ji Yuan.

“Sploosh!”

The silver fish dropped into the water sphere extremely accurately the moment it was formed, and the iron hook on it was shaken loose from its mouth with Ji Yuan’s skillful strength.

The boy frowned, retracted the jade ring flying in the air, and stared at Ji Yuan alongside the girl, watching the little silver fish swim in the water sphere, unable to escape.

“Who are you? How dare you come to the Green Jade Pool to steal the silver fish!”

Ji Yuan temporarily put away the fishing rod, set it aside, and then turned halfway to look at the two angry children.

“Could it be that this clear water pond is exclusively owned by your Yuhuai Mountain?”

As soon as Ji Yuan had seen the blue jade ring pendant, Ji Yuan knew where these people were from.

“You know that we are from Yuhuai Mountain but you still haven’t given us the silver fish?”

The boy spoke very childishly, as if he were no different from the children of ordinary people.

Ji Yuan also laughed.

“I’ve been fishing here for most of the day and only caught one fish. Even if you are from Yuhuai Mountain, and you are a famous immortal sect in Ji Province, you can’t just rob people, right?”

“You! This Green Jade Pool belongs to our Yuhuai Mountain! So the silver fish also belongs to us!”

“We come here every year to wait for the silver fish to appear, and it’s been several years!”

If it were the same Ji Yuan as in the past, perhaps he would have been truly stunned by these two kids, but he had now learned quite a few things.

“Haha, this Green Jade Pool is under no one’s jurisdiction, and is nearly 700 or 800 li2 away from Yuhuai Mountain. So now it has become your mountain sect’s property?”

After Ji Yuan finished speaking, he felt a slight movement in his heart, and shouted something behind the two children, half-joking and half-serious.

“Are there truly no elders here with you? Or are they just letting these two kids act unreasonably!”

Originally, this had just been a tentative remark from Ji Yuan with a hint of sarcasm, but unexpectedly, as soon as his words fell, a sound truly came floating through the air.

“I am sorry to have embarrassed your distinguished self, sir, it is indeed the fault of our Yuhuai Mountain!”

The voice was not as calm, deep, or powerful as Ji Yuan’s, but it could be described as gentle and elegant. As the voice rang out, a middle-aged man dressed in a cloud-patterned blue flowing robe and a jade hairpin in a bun on his head floated over, as if he had walked out of thin air.

Before this, Ji Yuan had not noticed any trace of this person’s existence at all. He could neither see nor hear him, which really scared him. However, after his experiences, Ji Yuan’s surface appearance did not change, and his eyes never wavered.

In fact, the person on the other side was also taken aback by Ji Yuan. He couldn’t see through who this fisherman was. He had no visible aura or divine light. He seemed to be just an ordinary mortal, blending in with the surrounding nature.

In particular, that move he made when he had been teasing the boy just now, when he twirled the fishing rod and the fishing line, was so effortless that it didn’t even give off the sense of being martial arts, and there was no trace of magical power. His water-control skill was also extremely delicate and smooth, without any awkwardness. He only used the simplest water-control technique, and nothing more.

And although the blue-robed man came out on his own, as he looked at Ji Yuan’s special eyes, he always felt that the other party could see through him even after he used the Invisibility Jade Talisman.

“Haha, I just shouted casually, but I didn’t expect that there would really be an elder following you. Sir, you are really patient!”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan also sat down and turned half of his body towards them, so that he didn’t have to keep turning his head to face the back. This also exposed the copy of “Bright Strategies” on his knees, which made the eyes of the man in blue narrow slightly.

‘Bright Strategies? It’s a book in Heavenly Script! ’

The newcomer didn’t take Ji Yuan’s words seriously at all. It was impossible for the other party to concentrate on reading a book while fishing.

“You must be joking, sir. It was these two young children who were making trouble. They are anxious because it takes a year for this Green Jade Pool to produce a Silver Essence, which would benefit the cultivation of my two juniors.”

After saying this, the man in blue waved his hand, and the two children were dragged directly to his side as if pulled by invisible threads. It seemed that he was disciplining the two children for their rudeness, but in fact, he had been quietly positioning himself to guard them.

The fisherman in front of him was of unfathomable skill. His gentle temper might not necessarily be genuine, so it was best to proceed carefully.

‘It’s just a Silver Essence, it would be better to find a reason to retreat! ’

“My name is Qiu Feng from Yuhuai Mountain. May I ask for your name, noble sir? Were you waiting here for us?”

Qiu Feng deliberately softened his tone, and changed his address to a more respectful one, and bowed with cupped hands towards Ji Yuan.

Of course, Ji Yuan didn’t dare to be arrogant. He slowly stood up and bowed in return. After hesitating for half a second, he decided to give his real name.

“My my name is Ji Yuan, there is no need to call me noble. I came here just because I read a A Historical Account from Outside the Tao in my spare time and learned that this place is where water spirits are born, so I was interested in looking around.”

‘It’s good that you were not waiting for us specifically! ’

Regardless of whether his words were true, Qiu Feng breathed a sigh of relief, and a smile appeared on his face.

“Since the gentleman has already caught the silver fish, we won’t stay any longer to disturb you. Let’s go! He’er, Yiyi, let’s go.”

As he spoke, Qiu Feng bowed once again to Ji Yuan, then turned around led the two children away.

Ji Yuan could see that Qiu Feng was cautious and wary towards him, but apart from bowing in return as he stayed by the pool, he didn’t know what to say. His first contact with a fellow immortal cultivator ended just like this?

The two children were still reluctant to leave, kicking rocks and weeds as they walked. After they had traveled a short distance away from the pond, the girl couldn’t but mutter a complaint in a low voice.

“What was that? You’re trying to steal our silver fish, and picking on kids…”

Qiu Feng didn’t know whether to laugh or cry. This child really thought that the Green Jade Pool belonged to her.

But he didn’t expect the boy to add another sentence:

“Oh, that stinky shameless…”

Originally, the first few words meant nothing, but as soon as the words “stinky shameless-” came out, Qiu Feng’s face changed drastically and he immediately interrupted him.

“He’er!”

These two juniors of his senior brother were too ignorant of the ways of the world.3 There were many people with profound cultivation in this world whose six senses were extremely sharp. Everything else was easy to let slide, but it was forbidden to directly insult someone to their face.

“Hahahaha… That does make sense. It’s truly shameless to grab something from a child!”

Ji Yuan’s frank voice came. Although he was laughing, it made Qiu Feng’s heart suddenly lurch. In his nervousness, he had already mobilized his spiritual energy, and the jade hairpin on his head turned from blue to red. He, an immortal in the Primordial Realm4 from a heavenly sect, actually felt helpless.

银 (yín): silver, 窍 (qiào): hole/orifice/key, 子 (zi): noun suffix

350-400 km, ~220-250 miles

Lit: “immensity of heaven and earth”

Generally, it goes: Foundation Establishment –> Core Formation –> Primordial –> Nascent Soul (and some people add stuff before, or split these into stages, etc.) So he’s pretty advanced in terms of cultivation.

Chapter 72: You Will Envy It

Ji Yuan was not an arrogant person, and he did not have the confidence to act rudely, but he understood Qiu Feng’s thoughts.

A silver fish condensed from water essence was naturally precious. At the very least, it was a treasure for Ji Yuan at the moment. Even if he didn’t know how best to use, it, it would probably be very nutritious if he used it to make soup.

But this was actually a bit of a waste, and besides, someone who actually knew how to use it was standing in the woods only a dozen steps away with an alert look on his face.

“Mr. Qiu, there’s no need to be nervous. It’s just a some angry words. I won’t argue with a child. Besides, when a child speaks rudely, the elders in the family are the ones who should teach him a lesson.”

Ji Yuan’s voice came gently. He was carrying a bag and an umbrella, with the green bamboo pole on his shoulder. He skillfully avoided the branches and vines on the mountain and walked slowly, like an ordinary person who didn’t even know any martial arts.

Interestingly, at the tip of the green bamboo pole, there was a sphere of water connected to a thin line of water that swung back and forth behind Ji Yuan, always managing to avoid branches by a hair’s breadth. It was fascinating and leisurely to watch.

It was a natural expression of Ji Yuan’s innocent and free-spirited side, which made all those who see it feel inexplicably peaceful.

As he walked closer, Ji Yuan, with an undiminished smile on his face, winked at the boy named “He’er.” The little boy didn’t dare say a word. He didn’t realize just how powerful Ji Yuan’s seemingly magnanimous words were just now, but he would definitely be taught a lesson when he returned.

Qiu Feng’s eye caught on the playful scene behind Ji Yuan’s back, and he felt much more at ease. He also secretly decided to let the boy suffer a little after they returned home. Since his senior brother couldn’t bear to discipline him, then he, as his junior brother, would have to educate the child properly!

Qiu Feng didn’t point the issue, nor directly teach the boy the lesson, but first asked Ji Yuan.

“I wonder what advice Mr. Ji might have for me!”

“Haha, I don’t dare to give advice, but I am really poor and short of money. Seeing that you like this silver fish so much, it would be a pity and a waste to use it to make my soup. Why don’t we exchange it for something else?”

If Qiu Feng had heard Ji Yuan’s request just moments earlier, he would have thought that this unknown expert was threatening him. However, he now did not have any such thoughts.

“I don’t know what you might want, sir, but if I can give it to you, I will naturally not be stingy!”

Qiu Feng thought that the other party’s kindness was valuable in and of itself.

Ji Yuan told almost no lies from beginning to end. He was truly penniless and had really considered making fish soup. The current situation couldn’t be any better.

Of course, he didn’t know the value of this Silver Essence, so he could only try to ask for what he wanted in his heart.

“I am just a country bumpkin, with limited knowledge and no need for anything too precious. I am merely extremely curious about things in the current world of immortal cultivation. Whether it be skills or techniques, strange books or stories, or small and wonderful methods and interesting toys, I would gladly exchange them for this Silver Essence. Well, even the most basic Qi training techniques are of great interest to me!”

Through Bright Strategies and A Historical Account from Outside the Tao, he now knew that there were no set stages in the basic stage of Qi training. There was no difference between the five elements, nor between yin and yang. Although each immortal sect had its own so-called Qi training technique, they were all actually more or less the same, with no differences. To put it bluntly, it was just common goods among the techniques of cultivating immortality.

What Ji Yuan desired most was precisely this common product.

This time was different from the previous misunderstanding with City God Song. Ji Yuan knew very well that Qiu Feng regarded him as an expert, but he did not point it out. It was easy to judge a person’s appearance, but not so easy to judge their heart. It was more appropriate at this time to let these people keep their awe of him, especially since there had been this small conflict of interest.

“Ah, ah! I have the basic immortal cultivation and qi training techniques. I have them! Master uncle, exchange them!”

The girl almost jumped with joy.

“Yiyi!”

Qiu Feng snorted, immediately shutting the girl up. What was the difference between exchanging these basic Qi training techniques and the man giving the fish away for free?

Thinking of the Bright Strategies book that he had just seen, and then recalling what Ji Yuan had said about the Historical Account from Outside the Tao, it was the first time that Qiu Feng had seen these kinds of ancient, hundreds-of-years-old compendiums. It seemed that this Mr. Ji was truly interested in strange things.

With this thought, Qiu Feng made up his mind. He must establish a good relation with this unfathomable master!

“Noble gentleman, I, Qiu Feng, understand your desires. Unfortunately, I have no rare treasures on me at this moment. However, I have an ancient chapter that you would definitely be interested in!”

After speaking, Qiu Feng waved his arm and a slender white jade stick flew out from his sleeve. It was crystal clear and about the length of one finger.

“This is a fragment of the Spirit Binding manuscript.1 I have studied it for over ten years. Although I have not gained any extraordinary skills from this study, it has helped me understand the skills I already have and concentrate my mind. I wonder if it might catch your eye, sir?”

As Qiu Feng introduced it with a few words, the white jade slip flew towards Ji Yuan, who caught it in one hand.

Spirit Binding? This was the strange technique mentioned in the original immortal cultivation book!

Even through it was just a fragment, Ji Yuan knew that this kind of thing was definitely not a cheap commodity.2 The silver fish was probably not nearly as valuable, but Ji Yuan was reluctant to decline, and it seemed Qiu Feng himself didn’t care.

But, although he was very happy, he didn’t know why, Ji Yuan still really wanted to practice the Qi training more…

“I’m afraid this Silver Essence is not worth nearly as much. Thank you, Mr. Qiu, for your kindness. I appreciate it.”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan waved his arm and swung the fishing rod. The sphere of water crossed over the fishing line like a fisherman reeling back a fish. With Ji Yuan’s superb strength and balance, the sphere of water flew in an arc steadily towards Qiu Feng.

The latter hurriedly cast a spell to stop the water sphere, but inevitably caused the water to splash a little. Now, even the boy and the girl could judge who was more skilled, and they knew it was incredible.

Qiu Feng sighed slightly in his heart. Apart from controlling water, the other party still did not show the slightest spiritual power. Meanwhile, he himself had to cast a spell to accept the water sphere, causing it to ripple.

“Mr. Ji, since this matter here is concluded, let’s say goodbye. If you get the opportunity in the future, you are welcome to visit Yuhuai Mountain as a guest!”

“Okay, I’ll definitely visit if I have the chance!”

Both parties were quite satisfied, and they bowed and parted ways together.

The sphere of water containing the silver fish was in the hands of the girl. The child also used her water-control skills to toss the ball up and down to tease the fish. At Qiu Feng’s urging, the two children followed him and left.

However, after walking about a dozen or so steps, the girl seemed to communicate something to Qiu Feng, and turned around and ran back towards Ji Yuan. When she got closer, she took out a slightly larger white jade slip from her sleeve pocket, and handed it to him.

“Senior Ji, this is my basic Qi Training technique. You just said you were interested in it, but this is all I have!”

After saying this, without waiting for Ji Yuan’s surprised reaction, the little girl tossed over the white jade slip and quickly ran back to Qiu Feng, who also bowed to Ji Yuan with a smile.

Ji Yuan’s heart was now filled with surprise. Between Qiu Feng’s guidance and the little girl’s ability learn how to behave, it meant a lot to Ji Yuan. He couldn’t help but laugh when he caught the white jade slip.

“Hahahaha… Wonderful, very wonderful. The girl’s name is Yiyi, correct? If you encounter difficulties in the future, I, Ji, will definitely try my best help you once. We will see each other sometime in the future, hahahaha…”

This laughter was full of energy and joy, and filled with Ji Yuan’s presence and confidence. Even though he just had a minor role today, he still had confidence in himself.3

Seeing Ji Yuan laugh and bow to him in return, Qiu Feng couldn’t stop smiling as he took the two children and walked away. After about a hundred steps or so, their figures slowly got smaller as they walked farther and farther away.

. . .

A moment later, Qiu Feng led the two children in riding the wind to fly in the sky, still smiling.

“Master Uncle, there’s no need to be so happy even though you got a fish!”

Seeing the boy open his mouth, Qiu Feng shook his head slightly. He looked at the girl, who was still playing with the water sphere, smiled, and answered the unstated question.

“Haha, there is a kind of wonderful and extraordinary person in this world who is extremely wise and free-spirited, innocent and simple, and who returns to nature. Getting to know them is a matter of fate. You don’t understand now, but in the future, you will envy it.”

拘 (jū): to capture/to restrain –> “Binding”, 神 (shén): god/deity/supernatural/magical/mysterious/spirit –> “Spirit”, 残 (cán): to destroy/to ruin/incomplete/remnant –> “Fragment”, 篇 (piān): sheet/piece of writing –> “Manuscript”

Lit: “definitely not a cabbage” No, really? XD

i.e. even if he was weak now, he had confidence in being strong enough to be able to keep his promise in the future

Chapter 73: Chess Piece Stealing the Pill

It had been a while since Qiu Feng and the two children had left. Ji Yuan waited until he was completely sure that they were gone. Then he returned to the Green Jade Pool in almost uncontrollable excitement, playing with the two white jake sticks, one large and one small, in his hand.

As expected of Yuhuai Mountain, they truly liked everything to be associated with jade. Whether it be the blue jade pendant, the white jade slips, or even Qiu Feng’s jade hairpin, Ji Yuan could detect the subtle movement of a mysterious light in all of them just now.

“Oh, I forgot to question them on behalf of Wei Wuwei!”

Ji Yuan slapped his head and laughed when he remembered this. After all, he was not very familiar with Wei Wuwei. If it had been for Xiao Yin Qing, he would never have forgotten to do it.

By the deep green pool, Ji Yuan sat down in the same place, put the green bamboo pole aside, crossed his legs, and began to read the contents of the two white jade slips.

This method of recording information had been mentioned in both Bright Strategies and A Historical Account from Outside the Tao. It was called the method of conveying the spirit through objects, and was a characteristic information-recording method for immortals. It was just more laborious to create, so it was not often used in normal situations. The material used could be gold, iron, stone, jade, or other things. The higher the scribe’s cultivation level and the stronger their spiritual power, the lower the requirements for the material. Even in the Historical Account, it was mentioned that there were people who could convey their spirit through objects like flowing water or the breeze, which could be considered extraordinary.

As for the two sticks of white jade in Ji Yuan’s hand, they were both white jade of excellent quality, probably due to Yuhuai Mountain’s wealth. Naturally, it was a good material, with the least requirements for the scribe’s cultivation.

After all, the Spirit Binding Manuscript was just a fragment. Qiu Feng had been studying it for over a decade and was still not able to restore it. Ji Yuan didn’t think he would be able to do anything with it right away, so he set it aside first and held the larger of the white jade sticks in his hand.

There were several characters engraved on it, with the words “Yuhuai Small Exercises,” and there were some engravings like waves of water on the front.

Reading this jade slip was much easier than reading the books in Heavenly Script. The person who touched it only needed to input some spiritual energy into the jade slip. There would naturally be a spiritual sense that drew in the mind and conjured up its detailed contents in their consciousness.

Even if it was only a basic Qi Training Art, the content of it far exceeded the original Qi Guiding Art. If one really wanted to compare, the Qi Guiding Art could only be regarded as a fraction of the content of the Qi Training Art, which incorporated many principles and explained the basics of cultivating immortality in detail.

There was a whole vast world all around him, and a whole small world inside of his body. The sun and the moon were connected to the stars and the acupoints. There was no end to the exploration of the world inside of the body, and its complexity was no less than the complexity of the wide world around him.

The Qi Training Art was based on connecting the five elements of the internal organs, to create combinations like heart fire, kidney water, and other pairs inside the body to refine spiritual energy. Based on Ji Yuan’s understanding, this was all very mysterious.

“It’s no wonder that most of those innate masters of the martial arts world die with regrets after reaching the peak. This kind of thing cannot be comprehended by just understanding internal skills and meridians.”

With a sigh, Ji Yuan began to practice refining Qi for the first time in his two lifetimes. The so-called small exercises were the first few stages to training Qi to cultivate immortality, collectively referred to as Nourishing Qi. Not only did one have to cultivate the two key Qi of heart fire and kidney water, one yin and one yang, but also refine and nourish the spiritual power.

However… Of the two biggest hurdles in cultivating immortality, the first being to perceive the spiritual energy and channel it as Qi into the body, and the second being to internalize Yin and Yang in the world inside of the body, neither held any difficulty for Ji Yuan.

His experience was completely different from how other immortal cultivators in the basic stage perceived their internal organs and visualized the world, as their visualized world could only be a room at most. Ji Yuan simply entered into a state of concentration and thought of transforming his body into the world. The whole world in his concentration was already vast.1

The mountains and rivers, rivers and lakes, the sun, the moon, and the stars all appeared one by one…

Ji Yuan did not linger on the beautiful scenery of the mountains and rivers, but rather quickly began to practice the key points, silently reciting the formula to help guide the spiritual power, and stimulating the vitality of his organs to link them to his inner world.

‘The heart fire appears. ’

A thought came to his mind.

Boom…

Above a giant peak somewhere in his world, a ball of fire burned from the sky like the sun. Ji Yuan could almost feel the infinite heat. He was not as excited as he had originally imagined he would be. Although he could experience emotions during his concentration, his self had been assimilated by his vast world, so his emotional fluctuations were as peaceful as the vast inner world itself.

‘The kidney water appears. ’

The next thought came to his mind.

Crash…

At the same position in the inner world, the rivers on the earth converged and the waves rolled.

The situation was quite different from what the Yuhuai Small Exercises described, or perhaps it had even reached a point beyond recognition. However, Ji Yuan felt that it wasn’t a bad thing.

‘Qi transformation of yin and yang! ’

One of the two most critical steps. The earth surged and the sky blazed. An infinite water force climbed up from the bottom, and a blazing flame rolled down from the sky.

One red force and one translucent force converged at the top of the tallest peak of the inner world with astonishing momentum.

Ji Yuan was like a bystander watching this awe-inspiring scene in front of him, unmoved. He watched the fire and water gradually vaporize and transform at the intersection, forming one white force and one black force.

These two colors gradually rotated at their point of collision, forming a strange vortex, like a huge Tai Chi map,2 but missing the two circular dots on either side.

‘The Yin-Yang furnace! ’

This was the most crucial step, and even Ji Yuan couldn’t help but have his heart race.

“Boom!”

Originally, everything in the inner world seemed to be silent, but at this moment, Ji Yuan seemed to hear a clear explosion, and the world seemed to shake.

At the top of the giant peak, in the center of Yin-Yang Tai Chi map, a huge pill furnace3 emerged among the infinite mysterious ripples.

“Huuu…”

Ji Yuan body in the outside world exhaled lightly, mainly because the description in Bright Strategies was just too scary. It kept cautioning some difficult things, saying that some people ignited the furnace thousands of times but still failed to pass the stage.

Until the furnace in his inner world was completed, Ji Yuan could temporarily rest assured. If the cultivator did not suffer extremely serious or bizarre injuries, the pill furnace in his body would not disappear. In the future, it would appear as long as he visualized it.

Ji Yuan visualized the towering alchemy furnace in his body as he stood on a mountain peak. No matter how he looked at it, his aptitude at this didn’t seem to be too bad.

A smile appeared on his face, and his mind raced freely. The spiritual energy around him seemed to disappear into thin air, then appeared in front of the pill furnace and was absorbed by the holes around the body of the furnace…

The night in Laohua Mountain passed quickly in silence. When it was almost dawn, Ji Yuan felt the first strand of red elixir Qi4 overflow, and then a steady stream of red Qi flowed out from the holes in the elixir furnace as if it were a stream of stars.

Originally, there was no need for Ji Yuan to take care of this step at all. This red Qi would seep out and transform his inner world, merging the acupoints and orifices between his physical body and his inner world. However, this barrier had already been passed. Based on the pill furnace, the orifices corresponded to the Sea of Qi in his physical body, which was the core of the Elixir.

In terms of immortal cultivation, Ji Yuan currently had immortals, the plan at this stage is about one mu5 of Dantian.6 Although the concept of the Dantian being the Sea of Qi in the martial arts terminology of this world, this Dantian was not the same thing. Their names were the same, but the core is completely different.7

But something that shocked Ji Yuan also happened at this time. Three chess pieces, one black and two transparent, swept across the visualized pill furnace like shooting stars. Before the red Qi had formed into spiritual power, 70% of it had already been taken away. The remaining pitiful 30% had to be forcibly introduced into his Dantian by Ji Yuan’s quick focus. Otherwise, there might not have even been a trace of it left.

Ji Yuan opened his eyes, stretched out his right arm, and made the sword-fingers hand gesture. A half-illusory and half-solid black chess piece appeared at his fingertips. It looked the same as before, as if the disappearance of the red Qi that Ji Yuan had worked so hard to refine had nothing to do with it. The same was true when he vanished the black piece and held the two transparent chess pieces.

Ji Yuan had mixed emotions. He wasn’t sure for a long time whether to cry or laugh.

“Hey… Forget it… After all, it’s all my own, so I can control it even if the pill was stolen. Anyway, the size of my Qi supporting world’s Elixir Core is not large…”

Well, the stolen pill was a cultivation term that Ji Yuan himself invented, with absolutely no other input.

At this moment, the sky had already turned to dawn. Ji Yuan exited the cultivation state and looked around. He found that he was completely surrounded by thick white mountain fog, and his clothes were extremely damp.

He patted his butt and stood up.

“Haha, let’s go!”

. . .

At this moment, in Niu Kui Mountain, nearly 300 li8 away from Laohua Mountain, in Lu Shanjun’s tiger den, a huge monster lying in the darkness suddenly seemed to feel something. A pair of enormous green tiger eyes opened in the darkness.

“Sir, you used ‘the way of man’ to refer to my former demonic path. Evil spirits are born from evil, which consumes my already inefficient spiritual power to increase my malice and karma. Over time, as demons are born, it is difficult to escape the tribulation. The way of heaven is the complementarity of yin and yang. My realm has increased a little! Hohohohoho…”

With a terrifying hoarse laughter, the tiger grinned with a terrifyingly large mouth, revealing pale fangs that were vaguely visible in the darkness.

At the same time, halfway up another peak of Niu Kui Mountain, a loud fox cry sounded in the morning sun; and, on the bed with his family in Ning’an County, Yin Zhaoxian’s sleeping frown relaxed a little.

T/n: a moment of silence for me please, who had to translate all of these cultivation terms T_T

The subtext is that because JY is blind, he’s already visualizing the world around him, so when he needs to visualize his inner world to cultivate, he can imagine the whole vast world since that’s what he’s lived with, while other cultivators can only imagine a room. Honestly, it’s a really cool way to work with what could be a character’s weakness, and turning it into a strength. Giving minor Toph Beifong vibes

The classic Yin-Yang symbol

If you’ve read cultivation/martial arts novels before, you would be familiar with this. Chinese alchemy was actually a thing, but it didn’t produce miraculous pills like you would see in various webnovels or manhua. The historical alchemy furnaces were complex systems of fans and pots and turning grinders, while the fantasy pill furnaces are more like a magic pot with a couple of ingredient slots. I’d recommend looking up images if you’re curious what these ones in the story look like.

丹 (dān): red/pellet/powder/cinnabar, works with the furnace. You may remember that “Vital Qi” was green, the color of life, and this Qi with the furnace is red (maybe because of the furnace’s relation to fire?). Could also be translated as “Elixir” apparently, so you’ll see them used interchangeably

~2.5 acres, a little under 2 standard soccer fields

Dantian also translates to “Elixir field” and “Sea of Qi,” hence both of those terms in the previous paragraph. It is believed to be the central seat of life force and energy.

It seems like here, as opposed to “standard” cultivators who increase their Sea of Qi in the Dantian, JY’s method is to have the pill furnace as the central Dantian and the inner world as his power

~150 km, ~100 miles

Chapter 74: A Magnificent Scene

He picked up his oil-paper umbrella and wrapped it up, then looked at the fishing rod beside him. It would be inconvenient to carry it, but it would be a pity to throw it away. After hesitating for a long time, Ji Yuan simply took off the fishing line and hook, and left the green bamboo rod behind by the pond.

Looking at this clear pond again, although it took a year to produce a silver fish, it could still be regarded as miraculous.

“Next time, I will definitely get a fish from here to make soup and see how it tastes!”

Muttering to himself, Ji Yuan stepped away from the edge of the pond.

At this moment, the Laohua Mountain was covered in fog, and one couldn’t see anything more than five meters away. However, this had no effect on Ji Yuan. On the contrary, since it was basically impossible for any mountaineer to dare venture around casually at this time, Ji Yuan’s pace became very fast.

From time to time, he would use the tree branches and cliffsides to push himself into the air, or he would move forward freely with the elegant and comfortable Dragon-Flying skills, his body occasionally swaying as if he were slightly drunk. In an instant, he had crossed a large area of rugged mountain roads.

While he journeyed, Ji Yuan not only compared some contents of Qi Training Art, but also thought about the changes and functions of the three chess pieces.

These three chess pieces appeared at the time when Lu Shanjun first sought the Tao; the moment when the red fox was released and kowtowed; and the morning when Master Yin read the farewell poem he had given him.

According to the understanding of people in this world, these occurrences were naturally unfathomable, but if he included the information from various imaginary stories Ji Yuan had seen on the Internet, it was not difficult to conclude that fundamentally, these three moments in time all had a huge impact on each of the three parties.

Among them, Lu Shanjun and the little fox were easy to understand. The only one who was a little strange was Master Yin. However, Yin Zhaoxian understood human nature. It was very likely that the letter aroused Yin Zhaoxian’s ambition, and perhaps this ambition was great enough to change his future path in life.

Thus, it seemed that the creation of chess pieces should be a corresponding indication of destiny.

People could believe in the existence of fate but not completely trust it; fate might indeed exist, but it was not necessarily unchangeable.

During this period of time, Ji Yuan had also come into contact with many other people, including the Nine Young Heroes a while ago, and Wei Wuwei more recently, but none of them had produced chess pieces. Perhaps their level was not high enough, or perhaps they themselves did not have the “capacity to become a chess piece.”

“Then why did Lu Shanjun’s original chess piece turn black?”

Ji Yuan muttered to himself. He thought of the evil creature in the well. It was his finger gesture that had severely damaged the evil creature, causing its demise and changing the color of the chess piece.

‘Was it due to the Yin and Water elements that the chess piece turned black, or was it due to the evil spirit, or something else? Does this affect Lu Shanjun? It seems to have a greater impact on me…’

At this thought, Ji Yuan waved his left sleeve, and a large swath of white mist with a radius of ten feet gathered together. In an instant, it had formed into a round and crystal clear sphere of water in Ji Yuan’s left palm.

‘My water control skills are indeed stronger than my fire control skills!’

The most important role of the three chess pieces before had been in assisting Ji Yuan in gathering spiritual energy through the Qi Guiding Art. The reaction of the three chess pieces in stealing the elixir just now caused Ji Yuan to think more deeply about it.

In the beginning, Ji Yuan had believed that, although the chess pieces could gather spiritual energy, they did not seem to truly want to absorb it. Now, it seemed that they were more eager for the red Qi produced by cultivation.

‘The red Qi, and the chess pieces consuming Qi, especially this precious first ray of red Qi… Does it have a greater impact on me, or does it also impact the person represented by the chess piece? ’

“Hey! Let’s brush teeth and wash up first…”

Ji Yuan smiled self-deprecatingly. How could a small character like him have such complicated thoughts? He stretched out his hand and broke off a branch from a vine, then started to rinse his mouth and brush his teeth with the help of the sphere of water in his left hand as he walked. Finally, he poured another sphere of water over his face and rubbed it.

. . .

When Ji Yuan exited Laohua Mountain, the sun had already risen high in the sky. As the sunlight shone, the fog in the mountain gradually dissipated.

Ji Yuan used the water-control technique, and the moisture on the clothes on his body immediately seemed to evaporate into mist in the sunlight, surrounding him. If someone happened to see it, he would look as ethereal as an immortal.

There was a village on the south side of the mountain pass, which was mostly inhabited by boatmen and fishermen. There was a small ferry port down the dirt road that connected to the mountain path. The Xiaoshun River was already sparkling in the morning sun.

As it was still early, there were almost no people coming over the mountains from Jiudaokou County. Many boats of varying sized were moored at the docks. However, a large ship that looked to be heading to Jiudaokou had just arrived. People were disembarking and boatmen carried things up and down the gangplanks. Several donkey carts and horse carriages were parked at the dock.

It wasn’t even the peak busy hour, but there were already signs of hustle and bustle.

Although Xiaoshun River had the word “small” in its name,1 it was not actually a small river. Its width ranged from 20 to 30 zhang.2 It led straight to the Chunmu River in the southeast direction, and was a key component of the nautical trade route of Jiudaokou County.

Ji Yuan nibbled on a leftover biscuit and walked to the ferry terminal at a normal pace. Without even looking at the big ships, he headed straight towards a small passenger boat with a tarp-covered awning. An old man in his fifties and a dark-skinned young man who looked to be old enough to be his son were cleaning the boat.

“Boatman, do you accept business to Chunhui Prefecture?”

Ji Yuan’s powerful voice sounded, attracting the attention of the two busy people on the boat towards the shore. A man wearing a wide-sleeved gray robe, carrying a bag and an umbrella, stood on the pier. He looked like a scholar, except for his hairstyle. At first glance, he looked to be in his thirties or forties, but upon closer inspection, he could have been younger. Not even the old boatman could tell how old this person was.

The old man walked to the bow of the boat and responded to Ji Yuan.

“Of course I can go there. Is the gentleman alone or with a companion? Would you like to charter a boat, or wait for other passengers to go with you?”

Ji Yuan thought for a moment before asking a question.

“I am alone. How much does it cost to charter a boat as opposed to waiting for passengers?”

“If you charter the boat, it would only take only three days to reach Chunhui Prefecture, since the weather is favorable this season. The fare will naturally be paid by you alone, which is 200 coins.”

Two hundred coins, which was around 1,200 wen, a little over one tael of silver. Ji Yuan frowned, thinking that this price was a little expensive.

“If you are waiting for a other passengers to go with you, sir, please wait here for a while. I will put up a sign to attract passengers, and write that we are headed to Chunhui Prefecture. You can also find people who wish to go there yourself. You can all share the boat fare, or you can pay a little more. As long as everyone agrees, please rest assured, sir. There are still many people going to Chunhui Prefecture every day, but my boat is small and can carry at most ten people. Otherwise, you will have no room to sleep at night.”

Ji Yuan looked at the boat. It was about 3 zhang long and 1 zhang wide in the middle,3 with a mast in the center. A black canvas covered the stern. This was probably the place for the passengers to take shelter from the rain and rest.

“Alright, sorry to bother you. I’ll go compare prices!”

“Please do so at your own convenience, sir. But the price of our boat is already very fair!”

After saying this, the boatman continued to clean up the cabin with the young man on the boat, seeming very confident.

Sure enough, after wandering around for a long time, Ji Yuan finally came back to that boat. It wasn’t that there were no cheaper boats, but considering the time required, cleanliness, and comfort, this one was really the most suitable.

Seeing him come back, the old boatman also smiled.

“Well, sir, have you made up your mind?”

“Well, boatman, we’ll wait for the first half of the day. It would be best if there were passengers joining us. If there are no passengers, then I’ll charter a boat.”

“Okay, sir, it’s up to you! For a three-day itinerary, the meals on board will be fresh fish, no extra fee required!”

This time, their tone became much more respectful. There were indeed people going to Chunhui Prefecture every day, but they all preferred large passenger ships. Their small boat didn’t get much business. Ji Yuan just didn’t like the noise of large ships.

After hanging up the sign calling for passengers to Chunhui Prefecture, Ji Yuan didn’t go out to solicit customers. Instead, he sat down at the prow of the boat and read a book, as if he did not care whether or not passengers joined him.

Moreover, Ji Yuan asked the boatman to set the fare at 120 coins for one ride, and he would cover the rest. It wasn’t that Ji Yuan was showing off, but rather that it was inappropriate to share the cost equally. It would be fine for people to spend less money and squeeze into a bigger boat.

By noon, only six people had gathered in total: two scholars who were traveling together, an old man accompanied by a younger man – a grandfather and grandson – and the last two were not related to each other, one strong man with a beard and a thin, middle-aged man.

The boatman only said that the fare was 120 coins, but did not mention that Ji Yuan would be covering the remaining fare, as requested by Ji Yuan in advance.

Seeing that the boat passengers basically didn’t greet each other, Ji Yuan also did not move from beginning to end, though he heard all of their voices. In this era, there were still few women traveling.

At noon, the boatman came to ask Ji Yuan’s opinion. After getting his approval, he untied the mooring ropes and set sail, pushing the big oar at the stern4 along the southeastern direction of the Xiaoshun River.

As the old boatman rowed the boat, he sang a loud fisherman’s song in a strong voice, following the rhythm of the oars. The cadence of the rhythm was particularly charming.

“Fishing boat~ Paddle up~ Fisherman~ Laughs aloud~”5

Ji Yuan, who had been sitting on the prow reading a book, smiled knowingly when he heard the song. He turned his head and looked towards the stern. As the old man sang, the color of his blurred aura and mood were slightly different from before. 6

Looking up at the sky, Ji Yuan felt inspired.

“The physical appearance of the human body is also like the appearance of the sky, ever-changing!”

小 (xiǎo)=small

~200-300ft, 66-99m

~30ft/10m long, 10ft/3.3m wide

Look up classical Chinese boats, with one long pole that serves to push the boat along, and used as a single standing oar – it’s not the same as a rudder, though it can function like one

T/n: don’t ask me what’s going on here, I can only guess that JY is finding “magic” in everyday acts

Chapter 75: Half a Pot of Rice Wine Into the Heart of the River

This kind of small boat, made entirely of wood, had a light hull, so when the waves were strong, it would feel as if it were swaying as it moved along the surface of the Xiaoshun River.

The places with the greatest degree of movement were at the bow and the stern, but Ji Yuan really enjoyed this sensation at the bow. Occasionally, he would put down his book and look at the scenery along both sides of the river bank, and watch other ships passing by.

Some time after traveling for a bit to leave the docks, the boat hoisted the sails. With the wind in his favor, the old boatman no longer rowed the boat, and merely needed to steer to control their direction. His fisherman’s song stopped.

The two boatmen, the old man and the youth, were indeed father and son. Their home was the village next to Laohua Mountain. Most people in this village made a living from this ferry port, and they fished when that business was not good enough, so there was no shortage of fishing gear on the boat. Ji Yuan considered borrowing some to go fishing if he had the chance.

About a quarter of an hour after the boat had set sail, Ji Yuan decided to leave the bow and went inside to sit.

The area of the boat under the arched black canvas awning was quite spacious. There was a wooden-framed cabin at the rear end for the boatmen’s miscellaneous items. The two rows of benches nailed to the hull were actually enough to seat more than a dozen people, but considering the space required to lay down to sleep at night, it was estimated that ten people at most could occupy it.

The other six passengers sat there separately. The two scholar companions occasionally chatted with each other in low voices. The little boy was leaning against his grandfather, drowsy.

Ji Yuan actually felt that there was something wrong with the atmosphere when he was outside just now. This feeling became more distinct when he approached the canopy. The reason should be the bearded man.

Although Ji Yuan’s vision always seemed as if it were covered by a thick frosted glass layer, he could see basic outlines, if not some less-detailed features. When the man had gotten on the boat before, Ji Yuan found that he was really burly, almost the same size as Wei Wuwei.

But the impression Wei Wuwei gave was that he was plump, while this man was merely strong, with a hint of alcohol on his body. Ji Yuan couldn’t tell whether or not his face looked fierce, but everyone else tried to keep their voices as low as possible.

If this man hadn’t been the last to board the ship, Ji Yuan really doubted that anyone else would have dared to board.

Anyway, if they had to spend three days together, this atmosphere was not good.

When Ji Yuan came in, several people subconsciously looked at him. Some people had thought that this person who had been reading books at the bow of the boat was a relative of the boatman’s.

Seeing that there was a large space on the bench to either side of the burly man, Ji Yuan casually sat down next to him.

“Did this brother just have a drink?”

The burly man looked at Ji Yuan in surprise, as if he wanted to confirm whether he was talking to him. Seeing that Ji Yuan’s face was indeed pointed towards him, he spoke in a rough voice.

“Before I left, my elders treated me to a banquet at the pier’s wine shop. I bought some home-brewed wine and drank a few bowls.”

“Oh, brother is from Jiudaokou County? You seem quite strong and muscular. Have you ever practiced martial arts?”

When Ji Yuan asked this, it seemed to stir the man’s interest, and his tone contained a hint of excitement.

“I, Li Daniu, am from Dongwei Village. I have had a strong physique since I was young. People in the village said that I could do as much farm work as an hard-working ox. Since I was a child, I have longed for a hero who could fly over the eaves and walls to take me in as a disciple, and teach me martial arts to protect good and eliminate evil… Unfortunately, this kind of encounter never occurred… and then…”

As he spoke of this story, the man became depressed.

“When I entered the county capital a few years ago, the old coach of the county’s martial arts regiment saw me and said that it was a pity; I was past the age of practicing martial arts and laying a foundation, and it would be difficult for me to obtain martial arts achievements in this life…”

The dream was shattered.

“Oh, oh, brother, don’t be discouraged. There is always a way out. As far as I know, there are still some martial arts techniques in this world that do not have age requirements. With brother’s innate conditions, you will not have to worry about being unable to achieve anything in the future.”

“Hehe, sir, there is no need to comfort me anymore. I have long since given up on this dream. At the moment, I am traveling to Chunhui Prefecture to visit my relatives. My uncle has a small business there and is in need of a strong man. I’ll save up some money, marry a wife, and live a peaceful life!”

This man, despite looking quite fierce and speaking in a loud voice, revealed a sense of simplicity and restraint. He was by no means a bad person, but rather a true and simple countryman who felt uneasy in the big city and longed for peace.

“Okay, may you find happiness!”1

Ji Yuan smiled, bowed slightly, and turned towards everyone else under the canopy.

“I plan to go to Chunhui Mansion to visit. We will all be in the same boat for the next three days!”2

Seeing Ji Yuan’s straightforward and calm demeanor, the slight fear of the burly man disappeared. The others also introduced themselves, and the atmosphere on the boat became a lot warmer as everyone began to chat with one another.

The two scholars were traveling for their studies. The old man and young child were going to attend the funeral of a relative who had passed away, and the thin middle-aged man only said that he had something to do in Chunhui Prefecture.

The old boatman’s son also came over to chat and listen to new things sometimes.

As the boat sailed on into the evening, the estuary3 of the river was within reach, and the much wider Chunmu River could be seen in the distance.

“Zhuangzi, get ready to lower the net!”

“Okay!”

Everyone sitting in the cabin could hear the boatmen shouting to each other. Several of the passengers came out to take a look with great interest, and saw that the younger boatman had already taken a net and brought it out to the side of the boat, near the bow.

“It’s easiest to catch big fish at the river mouth. Just wait, everyone, I’ll let you a delicious feast tonight!”

The old boatman steadied the boat from the stern, and shouted to the passengers with a smile, furling the sails.

The young boatman grabbed the large net in his hand, twisted his body at an angle, then suddenly exerted force and skillfully threw the net forward. It flew out and expanded into a large circle, covering the surface of the river.

“Crash…”

The sound of the net entering the water was very pleasant. Moments after the net sank, the boatman began to pull up the net rope with all his might.

“Clap pah pah… clap clap…”

The net was still only halfway drawn when the fish caught in it were already splashing around in the water.

“Wow! I’m really lucky today, there are several big fish! Who can give me a hand?”

The young boatman happily called out to the passengers on the boat. Ji Yuan, who was already very interested, walked straight up to him. The burly man Li Daniu also quickly stepped forward to help.

“Clatter…”

“Patter patter… Patter patter…”

After the net was pulled in, the fish on the front deck of the boat jumped about noisily.

“Hahahaha, a good harvest!”

“Everyone, just wait for my father’s specialty, fish head soup and steamed fish with dried vegetables!”

“Oh, that’s great, that’s great! There is fish to eat, some big fish to eat!”

The exclamations of the adults mixed with the cheers of the children.

A big black carp weighing more than 20 catties,4 a grass carp weighing 10 catties,5 as well as two bighead carp6 and some river prawns jumped around, making everyone on the boat excited. If they had chosen a large ship, there most likely would not have been such a scene.

A clay stove was lit at the stern of the boat to steam rice and fish, and make soup. Not long after sailing into the Chunmu River, the small passenger boat dropped anchor and began cooking dinner while the river was calm in the nighttime.

At this time, there was another large ship anchored at the edge of the river. Compared to the small boat, it was brightly lit, with constant noise and laughter. The faint sound of zithers and musical instruments could be heard, as if there were a party on the ship, with singing and dancing.

Although the boat on which Ji Yuan was located was simple, it still had a rear cabin. The black canvas tent was not transparent, and the front curtain could not block the wind. The light of the lanterns in the cabin swayed along with the river surface. The group of people who had been clearly separated during the day now ate at the same table in harmony.

The old boatman’s craftsmanship perfectly presented the delicious taste of the river seafood, particularly the steamed fish with black mustard greens, which did not require any ginger or green onions, only a little salt. Accompanied by home-brewed rice wine, it was delicious and not fishy, ​and the group of people enjoyed it very enthusiastically .

“Sploosh~”

There was an indistinct sound of water splashing. The others continued eating, but Ji Yuan turned his head towards the outside.

“Someone fell into the water! Someone fell into the water!!!”7

There were screams outside, and the other people under the black awning also heard it. They all went out of the cabin to see what was going on.

“It seems like someone fell into the water from that large ship?”

“Yeah, seems like it.”

“Oh, has that person been rescued?”

The large ship was about 100 zhang8 away from them. It was hard to see it clearly from a distance. All they knew was that it was chaos over there. In particular, Ji Yuan’s eyesight was truly terrible from so far away, all he could hear were shouts and screams.

However, because of his excellent hearing, he could clearly tell that the man who had fallen into the water had not been rescued yet. It seemed that he was a drunk young man from a wealthy family, and he didn’t know how to swim.

“Splash~”

“Splash~”

“Splash~”

…

In the distance, someone on the ship stripped off his clothes and jumped into the river to save the person in the water. However, the river surface was far from the deck of the ship and could not be seen clearly, and the range of the light from the lanterns of the people on the ship was limited.

“Oh my, a few more people jumped down, it seems that they truly haven’t been rescued yet!”

“Yes…”

He fell into the water while drunk and didn’t know how to swim. Ji Yuan shook his head. The man was out of luck, he was afraid that it would be difficult to save him. Unfortunately Ji Yuan was not an immortal with magical powers. Even if he used a small water control technique and jumped into the water to look for him, he would not be more helpful than a few boatmen who were good at swimming.

“Huh!?”

Suddenly, Ji Yuan’s eyes widened, and he stared at the river surface in the distance.

“The young master is there! The young master is there! He’s floating there, go and save him! Can’t that idiot see him? He’s right there!”

Someone on the boat screamed excitedly, and the boatmen in the water had to turn and swim back in the direction they had just passed. They actually saw the young man dressed in white floating face up in the water, so they hurriedly kicked their legs and swam towards him together, brining him over to the side of the ship.

Hearing the faint cheers coming from the big ship, the group of people on the small boat also breathed sighs of relief.

“It’s okay, it’s okay, the person was rescued!”

“It shouldn’t be possible to drown in such a short time.”

“That’s good, that’s good!”

“Well, let’s go back to dinner.”

“That’s right, let’s eat!”

Ji Yuan also returned to the cabin with them, but came back outside again under the pretext of needing to relieve himself.

In the cabin, the old boatman had just lifted the small rice wine bottle to add more wine to his bowl, but found that no wine came out, even after tipping it twice.

“Strange, did you finish drinking it all?”

“Ah? It’s gone, how much did you drink!”

“No worries, no worries, I still have some. Just go get some from the jar!”

……

The evening breeze was gentle. Ji Yuan came to the bow alone with a sphere of rice wine hidden in his sleeve. He waved his right hand forward, and the sphere of wine fell silently into the center of the river like a winding, tiny water dragon.

“I hope you will accept half a pot of fisherman’s rice wine!”

After speaking, Ji Yuan went straight back to the cabin and picked up wine jug, and poured himself some rice wine that the old boatman had brought out, as if nothing had happened. Meanwhile, a large black carp swam happily under the river surface, in the spot where the wine had been dropped.

知足常乐 (zhīzúchánglè): to be satisfied with what one has (idiom)

Yes, the idiom is the same in Chinese and in English. 😀 It’s fun since they’re all literally in the same boat crossing the river, and figuratively in the same boat since they’re all in the same situation of traveling to Chunhui with strangers

The tidal mouth of a large river, where the tide meets the stream. See also: mouth of the river.

1 catty = 500g, so about 10kg/22lbs. Black carp weigh on average 15kg/33lbs, but that’s nowadays… Still a decent sized-fish. Grass carp on average weigh 25kg/55lbs, 10 catties = ~5kg preeeetty sure this is a mistake and should be 50 catties instead… that would make ~25kg… or it could just be a really small fish

Bighead Carp

1000ft/330m

Chapter 76: Two Sides to Understanding

“Wait a minute, let me turn the fish over…”

One of the scholars saw that one side of the steamed fish on the plate had been eaten, and hurried to flip it over,1 but the words he used made the boatman and his son very unhappy.

“What nonsense! Turning the fish over? It’s turning it ‘right,’ scholar, the word you have just spoken is unlucky for those who make a living on the water!”

The tone of the old boatman’s explanation was obviously a little unhappy, but the scholar immediately figured out the connection and quickly apologized.

“Oh, hey, me and my big mouth!2 Don’t blame me, boatman, don’t blame me. I don’t understand the language of people living on the water, so I’ll punish myself by drinking a glass of wine!”

“Haha, scholar, you are just greedy! Let me take the fish spine away, no need to move it, just eat it like this!”

In the cabin, there was constant noise and laughter, mixed with the clear giggles of a child laughing. Occasionally, someone would eat fish too quickly and get a bone stuck in their throat, and the old boatman would come to their rescue with chopsticks.

The fisherman’s rice wine was not too strong and tasted good. In addition, there was plenty of fish and meat to go along with it tonight. It was the first time that everyone had come together, so they finished the meal in half an hour and but were not tired.

“Dad, I need to go to the bathroom!”

At this moment, the young boatman felt a tightness in his lower abdomen and wanted to urinate.

“Go ahead, go ahead, just aim a little farther, okay?”

“Sure!”

The young boatman agreed, put down his chopsticks, and left the cabin.

Although he had drank a lot of rice wine, its alcohol content was not high. In addition, the young man had a good physique due to working on the boat, so he did not feel dizzy when walking.

He stood steadily by the mast, close to the side of the ship, untied his belt, relaxed his body, and a line of water rushed into the river.

“Haah…”

He felt comfortable after relieving himself. However, as he was tying his belt on his trousers, the young boatman suddenly heard the splashing of water in the river on the other side of the boat. He turned his head to take a look, and saw ripples on the surface of the water to that side.

“Splash…”

The sound of water splashing came again, this time from the bow.

The boatman approached the bow of the boat nervously and stood on tiptoe to look around, but he still didn’t see anything other than the ripples on the water. He felt a little fearful and hurried back into the cabin.

The group inside was still eating and drinking, and the few people who saw the young boatman running back with with a bad expression on his face were confused as to what was going on.

“Dad… there seems to be a water lord…”3

The young man lowered his voice and spoke to the old boatman. “When I was peeing just now, it felt like the splashed underwater near the boat were too abnormal, which seems like some of the situations I’ve heard in the rumors.”

The old boatman’s expression suddenly became serious when he heard those words. He looked at the others and didn’t say anything. Then he grabbed a wine glass and walked out of the cabin.

Everyone was a little confused as to what was going on, but the old grandfather seemed to have thought of something, and pulled his grandson back to prevent him from going out to watch the fun.

In this world, there were warriors at the peak of the martial arts world who were looking to make a breakthrough, and there were people obsessed with searching for immortality, but very few had ever met gods or immortals. In truth, however, the strange terrifying stories of ghosts and monsters actually did not occur too far away from ordinary people.

Some people would have encountered them without even realizing it. Some maintained reverence through word of mouth, and some died inexplicably without anyone ever realizing it.

To put it bluntly, it was just a matter of seeking different things. Evil spirits coveted human bodies, souls, flesh, and yang energy, and ordinary people were very afraid of them; on the other hand, mortals’ pursuit of immortality and the gods was ultimately biased towards selfish desires, which was opposite the aims of those beings. Even if cultivators had desires similar to those who sought them, most would not bring themselves to get involved.

Even the City Gods, no matter how busy they were, would hear so many stories of greed, selfishness, and dirty deeds in the temple each year. They would be so fed up with them and so annoyed that they would not care about involving themselves unless it were necessary.

Also, the difference in each party’s perception of time was too great. Setting aside immortal cultivators and gods, even monsters and demons often had been practicing for many years. In addition, information did not circulate easily. The world was so large, but few knew much about it. There were few things that happened that were widely circulated, and even fewer that could be traced back after the event had occurred. Instead, local anecdotes and folk traditions were passed down orally in villages.

In the area around ​​Xiaoshun River and Chunmu River, the people who had been sailing on ships for many years had more or less all heard of or even encountered strange things on the water. The so-called “Water Lord” was a respectful name for water spirits.

Although the other people in the black canvas cabin on the boat were a little confused at first, they soon vaguely understood. They watched the old boatman carry the wine to the side of the boat and reach out to pour the wine into the river.

“If you don’t offend me, I won’t offend you. I’ll give you a glass of wine as a token of my respect. Water Lord, Water Lord, please take your leave!”

He poured the glass of wine and muttered a few words. Although he couldn’t see anything with the naked eye, he seemed to be able to see ripples on the water surface.

“Okay, let’s continue eating. As long as we don’t go into the water, we’ll be fine. When we come back, we can all go to the River God Temple of Chunmu River to pay our respects.”

Although no one saw what happened just now, it still gave some people goosebumps, and they all went back inside the cabin. Only Ji Yuan stayed at the entrance of the black canvas tent and squinted at the river.

A man like Li Daniu had strong qi, blood, and vital fire. He could probably swim even if there were a water ghost. Ordinary water ghosts couldn’t do anything to him. However, if he couldn’t swim, then if he encountered a water ghost then he would most likely sink to the bottom and drown, even if he could be saved.

But Ji Yuan knew very well that the creature under the water was not a monster, but rather a big greedy black carp.

“Haha, others are relieving themselves off the side of the boat, and you’re still here to beg for a drink?”

Ji Yuan said with a smile, and ripples appeared on the water surface again. The big black carp swam away.

‘If only all spirits were this cute! ’

. . .

In the early morning, the boat’s passengers woke up to the occasional slight shaking of the boat, and found that it was already dawn. Ji Yuan had long been sitting quietly at the bow, while the old boatman had woken up before dawn and set sail once more. He would have breakfast, then take a nap and let his son take over.

Ji Yuan didn’t have a book in his hand at the moment, but instead held another white jade slip in his sleeve, which was the fragment of Spirit Binding manuscript given to him by Qiu Feng.

The so-called magical method of binding the spirit had two meanings. One was for the practitioner himself to concentrate his mind to assist in his practice. The other meaning was even more powerful, in Ji Yuan’s opinion, in that it could actually “bind the spirit.”

In his previous life, when he had read Journey to the West, Sun Wukong had asked, “Where is the old God of the Land?” and the Land God had appeared to greet him.4 This was the meaning of the second aspect of the concept of spirit binding.

However, this kind of thing was also very taboo. People who had this ability must use it in accordance to the actual situation and occasion. To put it simply, if Ji Yuan truly learned all of the skills of Spirit Binding right now, then stood on the boat and wanted the River God of the Chunmu River to come greet him…

Yes, he would have successfully committed the ultimate act of seeking death. He estimated that the River God’s body would not be affected at all. Instead, he would become angry and send a big wave to beat Ji Yuan to death.

What were the occasions for which this skill was truly suitable?

For example, some mountains, rivers, lakes or other beautiful places often gave birth to some magical creatures. Through talent and efforts, they would more or less have a connection the the earth and water veins. They would barely be regarded as gods, ones who had not officially been enthroned, and would be worshipped in land temples in small villages.

“Mr. Ji, come and have some rice porridge!”

The young boatman shouted from the cabin, interrupting Ji Yuan’s thoughts.

“Coming!”

After a response, Ji Yuan brushed himself off and stood up to go have breakfast together.

With a bowl of white porridge and a handful of black mustard greens on top, Ji Yuan walked out side the cabin again, holding his bowl and chopsticks. He stood there to let he breeze help cool the porridge, blowing on the surface of the bowl and scraping the porridge with his chopsticks to eat.5 Even though the boat occasionally shook, his body remained stable.

With the help of the southeast wind, the small boat Ji Yuan was riding was slowly overtaking the large ship due to its lighter hull. At the moment, the two ships were only a dozen zhang6 apart.

Many people on the large ship looked at the small boat and saw several passengers on the boat drinking porridge from their bowls, as the people on the boat also looked at the large ship.

A young man in white was lying on the side of the ship, staring at the small river boat in a daze. He saw the gentleman in gray standing against the wind and looking out over to where he lay. If he were not holding a bowl, the man would have formed a natural harmony with the boat and the river.

If you have a large fish, you have the spinal cord and ribs in the center going from the top of the fish down to the bottom; the parts you eat are to either side, so when it’s lying down on the plate half of the fish is above the spine – which they’ve eaten – and the other half is beneath the spine, so you have to flip the fish over to get to it.

Lit: “look at my mouth”

水 (shuǐ): water/river, 公 (gōng): highest order of nobility/honorable (gentlemen)

Don’t quote me on this exact line translation, feel free to go look up Journey to the West and read it yourself! It’s quite good. Lots of story arcs, some character development, Sun Wukong kicking a**, dragons, strong female characters, etc. Or you can watch these fantastic summary videos by Overly Sarcastic Productions 😀
